The commands keep their ids (`editor.lang`, `editor.clearLang`, ...) and their keys; each item carries `aria-keyshortcuts` and shows the key the command answers to now (Settings > Shortcuts changes included). The button joins the toolbar's roving tab stop (arrow keys, Home, End, mirrored in RTL) as its last stop; Escape closes the menu and returns focus to it, and a choice runs after the menu has closed, so a dialog it opens (Language, Image details) hands focus back to the text. Image details moved from the bar into the menu to make room: the 18th button made the bar wrap at the usual window width (editor column 736 px at 1280 px), while the bar with the new button and without it fits one row at 1280, 1024 and 768 px and scrolls sideways, as before, below `md`. Scene break moved the same way when the alignment button took the free slot a few days later (see "Text alignment"): the bar has room for 18 buttons at 1280 px and no more, so every addition after that trades one bar item for one menu item.

**Why:** WCAG 3.1.2 asks that the author can find the language command; a command that exists only in the native menu and the palette is invisible to anyone who works from the toolbar, and a button that is only an icon, with a short list, is the usual pattern for rarely used items. It also gives the less common marks and blocks a place on the screen.

**Rejected:**

| Rejected                                                  | Why not                                                                                                                                                    |
| --------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| One more toolbar button for the language command          | The bar wraps to two rows at the usual width, which moves the editor and broke e2e specs that click its centre; the same would follow for each later mark. |
| Leaving the language command menu-only                    | Not discoverable from the toolbar; the native Format menu is absent in the web build and on a phone.                                                       |
| Making the bar wrap (and fixing the specs)                | Two rows of icons cost writing space on every screen to show buttons that are rarely used.                                                                 |
| Smaller buttons or tighter padding to fit one more button | Changes the target size and the drawn design for the sake of one control; the slack is a few pixels and breaks with the next addition.                     |

**Follow-ups / caveats:** the pen file and `docs/design.md` still draw the toolbar without the button and with Image details on the bar; a pen frame for the button and its menu is needed (the pen is not changed by this work). The voiceover specs (`e2e-voiceover`) were edited for the new route to Image details but not run.

## Accessibility of the packaged app (Windows, macOS, Linux)

Tauri has no accessibility layer of its own: the page is read through WebView2, WKWebView or WebKitGTK. We test the web build in automation (axe, IBM Equal Access, Guidepup with real VoiceOver) and keep per-system checklists for the packaged app ([platform-checks-windows.md](platform-checks-windows.md), [platform-checks-macos.md](platform-checks-macos.md), [platform-checks-linux.md](platform-checks-linux.md)). The window title is set per screen and book with `useWindowTitle` (`core:window:allow-set-title`).

Decision: we do not add the `UiaProvider` WebView2 flag reported in tauri-apps/tauri#15624 up front. It is one unconfirmed report; the Windows checklist has an activation check (WIN-1 to WIN-7) and the flag is the fallback only if that check fails. Rejected: applying it now (an untested browser flag could change behaviour for every Windows user), and a `tauri-driver` axe smoke test for now (needs downloads, pre-alpha, no macOS support).

## Tooltips instead of native `title` (2026-11-03)

**Decision:** icon buttons show their label (and a shortcut, via `hint`) in `ui/feedback/Tooltip.tsx`, built on `@radix-ui/react-tooltip` (already a dependency): it opens on hover and on keyboard focus, Esc closes it, the pointer can move onto it without it closing, and it has no animation so reduced motion needs no special case. `aria-label` stays the accessible name and no interactive element carries a `title` (ratchet: `e2e/a11y-hover-hints.spec.ts`). Other hints became real text: reorder grips point at an sr-only sentence with `aria-describedby` (`ui/GripHint.tsx`), the estimated page number and the page-size chip carry sr-only text, long values wrap instead of being truncated, and Undo and Redo carry `aria-keyshortcuts`. Interface text is never below 12 px (`--text-micro` is 0.75rem; `e2e/a11y-text-size.spec.ts`). Tone is shown by a shape as well as a colour: Badge, Notice and CheckList icons differ per tone, find hits, PDF hits and History insertions and deletions use outline, dash, underline or strikethrough.

**Why:** WCAG 1.4.13 asks that content shown on hover or focus can be dismissed, hovered and persists; the native tooltip is none of these, is not shown on focus and not on touch. 1.4.1 asks that meaning is not carried by colour alone.

**Rejected:**

| Rejected                               | Why not                                                                                                                         |
| -------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Native `title`                         | Not keyboard reachable, not dismissible or hoverable, delay and styling uncontrollable, inconsistent across the three webviews. |
| A custom hover popover written by hand | Reimplements focus, Esc, collision handling and hover bridging that Radix already tests.                                        |

**Follow-ups / caveats:** Segmented items lose their `title`; their visible text is hidden only below the `sm` breakpoint, where there is no hover anyway. Badge adds an icon but no sr-only tone word because its text (Saved, Stale) already carries the meaning. The sighted keyboard user no longer sees the long page-size chip explanation (sr-only only).

## Typewriter mode (2026-11-03)

A basic version existed (`typewriterScroll` in `state/view/editorPrefs.ts`, a Settings switch and a private `centerCaret` in `Editor.tsx`). This section is the design it is rebuilt to: a pure, tested scroll target, a slack at the end, reduced-motion handling, and the same setting reachable from View, the palette and a shortcut. The preference keeps its stored name `typewriterScroll` so saved settings still load.

**Decision:**

- **Measuring.** The caret line is `view.coordsAtPos(selection.head)` (viewport coordinates of the line box; `head`, so Shift+arrows follow the moving end). The scroller is the nearest `.overflow-y-auto` ancestor of the editor DOM: `data-testid="page-scroll"` in `PageFrame` (Page view) and `reflow-scroll` in `ReflowFrame` (narrow reflow). Page view scales the stack with a CSS `transform`; `coordsAtPos` and `getBoundingClientRect` both return post-transform viewport pixels, so the difference "caret middle minus scroller middle" is already in scroll pixels at any zoom and no zoom factor is applied. Nothing is virtualised in Write mode (Read mode is, and is not affected). The pagination plugin may move text by a few pixels after a transaction (page spacers), so the scroll runs in a `requestAnimationFrame` after the selection update, measured then, and a newer update cancels an older frame. The pure function `lib/reading/typewriterScroll.ts` takes `{ caret: {top, bottom}, view: {top, bottom}, scrollTop, maxScrollTop?, tolerance? }` and returns the new `scrollTop` or `null` (inside the tolerance of 2 px, or clamped to where it already is). The view rectangle is the scroller's, clipped at the bottom to `visualViewport` so a phone's on-screen keyboard does not hide the middle.
- **When.** Only for a caret moved by keys: typing, Enter, Backspace, arrows, Page Up/Down, paste and undo. The editor stamps the time of the last `keydown` in the editor (as before); a selection change within 250 ms of it scrolls. A mouse click, a find jump, an outline jump, footnote and image dialogs (their keys are not typed into the editor) and a comment-style programmatic selection never scroll, so the author's own choice of where to look wins. Wheel and touch scrolling change `scrollTop` but not the selection, so manual scroll is left alone until the next key, which brings the caret line back (the behaviour of writing apps; there is no "lock").
- **When the pages change height.** In Page view the exact page breaks from Typst arrive about a second after the first estimated layout and move every line. `useTypewriterRefollow` (`editor/typewriter.ts`) watches the page stack's size and, while the editor has focus, puts the caret line back in the middle; the e2e specs showed the line left thousands of pixels away without it. A widened key window was tried first and did not help, so the 250 ms window stays.
- **When the browser moves the scroll position (2026-11-16).** Chrome's scroll anchoring moved the page by about 3,600 px a moment after typing at the end of a long chapter (a `scroll` event with no `scrollTo`, no resize and no key), and nothing re-centred the line until the next key; the arrow-key e2e spec failed on every attempt in CI. `useTypewriterRefollow` also listens for `scroll` on the pane and follows the caret when the editor has focus, unless the scroll is the author's own: a wheel or touch within the last 600 ms (`USER_SCROLL_WINDOW_MS`, momentum scrolling goes on after the last event) or a held pointer (the scrollbar being dragged). Keys are not counted as the author's scrolling: a key that scrolls moves the caret as well, and following the caret is then the right answer. Following a scroll that already put the line in the middle changes nothing (the tolerance), so it cannot loop.
- **Motion (WCAG 2.2.2, 2.3.3).** Scrolling is instant (`behavior: "instant"`), whatever `prefers-reduced-motion` says, so there is no animation to switch off. Smooth scrolling was built first and measured in the e2e spec: it restarts on every key and lags behind fast typing (after a burst of typing the caret line was still 266 px from the middle), so it is not offered even for people who allow motion. Nothing blinks or animates on its own, and the page moves only after an author action. The caret and focus are never moved: only `scrollTop` changes, so a screen reader's caret tracking (which follows the selection, not the scroll) is unchanged.
- **End slack.** While on, the scroller gets bottom padding of half its height (`typewriterSlack(height)`, kept current by a `ResizeObserver` in `useTypewriterSlack`) so the last line can reach the middle. It is padding on the scroller, not content, so it is not in the book and not in the page count; it is removed when the setting is off.
- **Near the top.** Accepted: the first lines of a chapter cannot reach the middle (`scrollTop` cannot be negative) and stay where they are until the text has grown past the middle. No top padding: it would push the first page card away from the top edge for everyone who has the setting on and make the first screen look broken.
- **IME.** Nothing scrolls while `view.composing` (the candidate window is anchored to the caret and moving the page under it is jarring); `compositionend` counts as a key, so the line settles when the text is committed.
- **Find bar, dialogs.** Their inputs have focus, so no keydown reaches the editor and nothing scrolls; find results scroll by their own code. Focus mode: unchanged, the same scroller. Read mode: not applicable (read-only editors, no caret). Tablets and phones: same code in the reflow scroller; the visual viewport clip is the only phone-specific part (soft-keyboard behaviour is untested on real devices). RTL: scrolling is vertical only, so nothing mirrors; the coordinates are the same.
- **Setting and commands.** `typewriterScroll` stays in `editorPrefs` (app preference, persisted, never in the project, off by default). UI: the existing switch "Typewriter scrolling" in Settings, Editor (a named switch, keyboard reachable); View > Typewriter Scrolling (command `view.typewriter`, ticked when on, shortcut `CmdOrCtrl+Alt+T`, available in Write mode like Focus Mode); the command palette lists it by itself because it is built from the menu model. The shortcut list in Settings > Shortcuts shows it by itself for the same reason (so it can be rebound). No clash: `Mod+Alt+T` is unused by the editor, the menus and the global keys.

**Why:** a caret that sits at the bottom of the screen for a whole chapter is the common complaint with long-form writing; centring is cheap (one rect read per key) and is independent of the document model. Making the target a pure function keeps the awkward cases (tolerance, clamping, near top) testable at 100 % without a browser. Measuring in viewport coordinates avoids having to know the Page view zoom or the card geometry.

**Rejected:**

| Rejected                                                           | Why not                                                                                                                                                  |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| ProseMirror `scrollIntoView` with `scrollMargin`/`scrollThreshold` | Only scrolls when the caret nears an edge; it cannot hold the caret at the middle, and its margins apply to the whole editor props, not to this setting. |
| Scroll on every selection change                                   | A click or a find jump would drag the page away from where the author just looked; breaks "respect manual choice".                                       |
| Smooth scrolling (also only without `prefers-reduced-motion`)      | Restarts on every key and lags behind fast typing (measured: 266 px off after a burst); instant needs no motion preference at all (WCAG 2.3.3).          |
| Top padding so the first lines can be centred                      | Shifts the first page card down for everyone with the setting on; the first screen of every chapter would look broken to save a few lines.               |
| A spacer node or decoration for the end slack                      | It would be in the editor's DOM (screen readers, pagination measure, the page count); padding on the scroller is outside all of them.                    |
| Scale correction by the Page view zoom                             | Unneeded: `coordsAtPos` and `getBoundingClientRect` are both post-transform.                                                                             |

**Follow-ups / caveats:** pen frames are needed (not drawn here): (1) Settings, Editor with the Typewriter scrolling row (exists as built; check the pen has it), (2) the View menu with the new "Typewriter Scrolling" item ticked and unticked on desktop, (3) the Typewriter Scrolling row in the command palette with its shortcut, (4) Page view and the narrow reflow with the setting on and the caret line at the middle, with the end slack visible at the end of a chapter. Not verified: a real soft keyboard on iOS/Android, WKWebView and WebView2 `visualViewport` behaviour, and IME composition in a native build.

## Vertically centred pages (2026-11-03)

### Decision

Short front and back matter pages can sit in the vertical middle of the page: the content block is centred between the top and bottom margins of the trim-size page. The title page, dedication and epigraph are centred by default in every built-in template; every other matter kind starts at the top. The author can override this per page. Centring applies wherever a page is drawn with a page height: Page view, Read mode, the PDF and the EPUB. Word, RTF and Markdown have no page layout and are unaffected.

- **One rule:** `resolveVerticalCenter(template, kind, entry)` in `packages/templates` (like `resolveDropCap`): the page's own `verticalCenter` (`true` centred, `false` top), else the template's default for the page's kind (`print.matter.verticalCenter`, a list of matter kinds), else false. Chapters (`kind` undefined), the contents page and the copyright page never centre, whatever is set. There is no book-level default: a book either follows its template or says what one page does.
- **Template data:** `print.matter.verticalCenter: MatterKind[]`, validated in `registry.ts` (an array of distinct matter kinds, no `contents` or `copyright`; those two are layout pages of their own). Classic Novel, Nonfiction, Modern Memoir and Poetry all list `title-page`, `dedication`, `epigraph`, which is what the PDF already did without a choice, so existing books render as before.
- **Manifest:** `verticalCenter?: boolean` on a front or back matter entry, refused on chapters, on `contents` and `copyright` pages (they have a layout of their own) and for any value but true or false. It is a visible setting an older app would drop on its next save, so `formatVersion` goes 8 to 9 with a no-op migration, a backup and a v8 contract fixture (`fixtures/contract/manifest-v8.json`), as `dropCap` did for 7 to 8. `setMatterVerticalCenter(m, id, value | null)` in `packages/format/src/edit.ts` sets or clears it; `EntryRow` carries it.
- **PDF (Typst):** a centred page is the existing `align(center + horizon, ...)` block (title page) or `align(center + horizon, block(width: 78%, ...))` (dedication, epigraph) inside a `pagebreak(weak: true)`; a page set to Top uses the same block with `top` instead of `horizon`. Typst centres inside the margins, and a block taller than the page flows on to the next page without being centred (alignment of an overflowing block has no free space). The marks (`matter-marks`) stay inside the aligned block, so page measuring and word marks are unchanged. Which pages use `centered-page` stays by kind; the choice only changes `horizon` to `top`. Other kinds that the author centres use the same `centered-page` wrapper, so their running header rule (off, as on dedication and epigraph) applies. `PdfChapter.verticalCenter` is the author's choice; `bodyOf` resolves it with the template.
- **EPUB:** reflowable, so there is no page height to centre in. The exporter adds the class `vcenter` to the body of a page that resolves to centred (`EpubChapter.verticalCenter` for the author's choice, `BuildOptions.centerKinds` for the template's kinds, like `dropCap`). The stylesheet has two layers: a fallback that works everywhere (a top margin in `%`, which every reading system resolves against the width, as the title page and dedication had before) and, inside `@supports (display: flex)`, a flex column with `justify-content: center` and `min-height: 90vh`, which resets the fallback margin. Reading systems that honour it centre the block in the viewport; the others keep the fallback margin; a page longer than the viewport simply grows (min-height, not height) so nothing is clipped and no extra blank page appears. A Top page has no `vcenter` class and no top margin hack. `body.title-page h1` loses its 30% margin: that margin now belongs to `body.title-page.vcenter`. The CSS has no remote resources and uses nothing EPUBCheck refuses (checked with `/validate-epub`).
- **Page view and Read mode:** both draw a page through `PageStack`, which now takes `verticalCenter`. When the page's cards come to exactly one page, the stack gets the class `page-centred` and `useCentredColumn` keeps `--page-centre-top` at half of the free height of the text box, so the block's middle sits on the text box's middle; the text column, indents, drop cap and horizontal alignment are untouched, only the column moves down. The height is measured from the content blocks (the page-break spacers `.pv-gap` skipped) and kept up to date by a `ResizeObserver` on the editor; the content's height does not depend on the offset, so there is no feedback loop. The pagination's measuring pass reads the editor with the `pv-natural` class, which turns the offset off, so block tops are measured as they flow and centring can never change a page break. The offset is `0` when the content fills the text box, so a page that fills it (or needs a second page, where the class is off anyway) is not shifted and not clipped. The blank verso the book puts before a dedication or epigraph is a card of the stack but not a page of the entry, so it does not count towards the one page. It is also a page-break spacer at the top of the column, and the cards are placed from the spacers' bottoms: the offset therefore goes below it (a `margin-top` on the page's first block, class `page-centred-after-blank`) instead of as top padding, which would carry the blank page and every card down with the text and leave the page uncentred. Centring starts at the first real layout, so the first measurement never runs with an offset above a spacer. The same CSS is used at any zoom (the stack is scaled by `transform`). Reading order does not change: the DOM order is the same, only layout moves it.
- **Right to left:** vertical centring does not depend on direction. A right-to-left title page keeps its inside margin on the right, as before. The EPUB rule has no left or right in it.
- **Overflow:** more than one page: no centring in Page view, Read mode and the PDF (Typst gives the block its natural size, flowing across pages); the EPUB grows past `min-height`. At 200% zoom and 320 px the Page view and Read mode scale the whole page, and the phone layout (`ReflowFrame`, plain reading text) never centres, so nothing is clipped (WCAG 1.4.4, 1.4.10).
- **UI:** the chapter list's row menu for front and back matter pages gets a "Vertical position" radio group with Default, Centred, Top, like the chapter's "First letter" group, hidden for the contents and copyright pages (a generated list, the imprint at the foot). The row's change goes through the book history (`actions.setVerticalCenter`, undo and redo) and is announced in the live region ("Vertical position of Dedication: centred"). The Templates page notes which pages each template centres. MCP and the app's exports pass the same resolved values as for `dropCap`.

### Why

Title pages, dedications and epigraphs are one short block that looks wrong at the top of a printed page; the PDF already centred them, but Page view and Read mode showed them at the top, so the author could not judge the page while writing, and the EPUB approximated it with a magic margin. One rule, in `packages/templates`, keeps the five outputs agreeing. A per-page override is needed because the right answer depends on the book (a long epigraph, a title page that is just one line). One measured offset in Page view and Read mode degrades to "no centring" when there is no room and cannot move a page break, because the measuring pass turns it off.

### Rejected alternatives

| Rejected                                                       | Why not                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| -------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Measure the block and add padding-top in the pagination plugin | Needs a second measure after every edit and the height of the block depends on the padding in some browsers; the flex layout does it with no JavaScript and no feedback loop.                                                  |
| `justify-content: center` alone                                | Overflowing content is cut off at the top (negative free space); auto margins fall back to zero. (`safe center` is not in every webview we ship.)                                                                              |
| A book-level default as well                                   | No author asked for it; a template already carries the default and a page the exception. Adds a second place for the same choice.                                                                                              |
| A new matter kind for centred pages                            | The choice is about layout, not meaning; it would double the kinds and the EPUB `epub:type` semantics.                                                                                                                         |
| `min-height: 100vh` and flex only in the EPUB                  | A page whose body has a margin overflows by that margin and some reading systems then add a blank page; 90vh with the fallback margin avoids it. A flex-only rule shows a top-aligned page in reading systems without flexbox. |
| A boolean on the chapter too                                   | A chapter opening has its own sink and ornament; centring a chapter is not asked for. The manifest refuses it, which keeps the setting from being silently ignored.                                                            |
| No version bump                                                | An older app would drop the choice on its next save.                                                                                                                                                                           |

### Follow-ups

- Pen frames to draw (not drawn here): "Vertical position" radio group (Default, Centred, Top) in the matter row menu, for the chapter list in light, dark and RTL; a centred title page frame for Page view (single page, content block centred between the margins); a note line on the Templates page for the pages a template centres. Roadmap line added.
- The EPUB centring is checked with EPUBCheck and Ace only for validity; how it looks in a real e-reader (Apple Books, Kobo, Kindle) needs a person with those devices.
- A part or section page kind (centred by nature) and "About the author" are not centred by default; the author can centre them with the per-page choice.

## Pages that start on the next page (2026-11-03)

### Decision

A front or back matter page can be set to start on the next page, even when the book's rules would put it on a right-hand page with a blank left-hand page before it. The blank page is the book's, not the author's: it holds no text, cannot be typed in or deleted, and Write mode drew it as an empty card the author could not get rid of. The choice (`startOnNext`) is per page, from the row menu, part of book undo and redo, and it changes the printed book, not just the screen.

- **Manifest:** `startOnNext?: boolean` on a front or back matter entry, refused on a chapter and for any value but true or false. An older app would drop the setting on its next save, so `formatVersion` goes 9 to 10 with a no-op migration, a backup and a v9 contract fixture (`fixtures/contract/manifest-v9.json`), as `verticalCenter` did for 8 to 9. `setMatterStartOnNext(m, id, value | null)` in `packages/format/src/edit.ts` sets or clears it; `EntryRow` carries it.
- **A page break inside a chapter:** `pageBreak` is a block node of the chapter document (`packages/editor-schema`), inserted from Insert → Page break or the toolbar's More formatting menu (both run the `editor.pageBreak` command the toolbar registers). It holds no content and prints nothing of its own. `paginate` (`apps/desktop/src/lib/page/pageLayout.ts`) starts the block after it on a new page (`kind: "pageBreak"` in `pagination/core.ts`); a break that lands at the top of a page adds no page, and two in a row leave an empty page — the same two-sided-leaf rule as the blank page rows, without a row to keep in order. Typst writes `#pagebreak(weak: true)` (a weak break does nothing at the top of a page), Word writes `<w:br w:type="page"/>`, Rich Text `\page`, the EPUB `<hr class="page-break"/>` with `break-after: page`, Markdown leaves it out and the audiobook counts it as skipped. In Page view the node takes no room (`height: 0`), so the break is exactly the card edge the PDF has; in Write view it is a thin PAGE BREAK rule the author can select and delete.
- **One layout rule:** `layoutBook` in `packages/shared/src/pageNumbering.ts` takes `startOnNext` through `PageEntryInput` and skips the right-hand-page push for that page, including the push of the page that restarts the arabic count (that page then starts on a left-hand page, with the count still starting at 1 there). Every surface reads that one function: the Write and Read page cards, the status bar, the Contents estimate and the print preview.
- **PDF (Typst):** `bodyOf` in `packages/export-pdf/src/typst/matter.ts` leaves out the `#pagebreak(weak: true, to: "odd")` before a page that says so, and passes `odd: false` to `#chapter-start`, which the preamble now accepts (`odd: true` by default, so every other page is generated exactly as before). The wrappers that give a page its layout by kind (`title-page`, `copyright-page`, `contents-page`, `centered-page`) do a weak break that starts nothing new, so leaving the break out is enough for them; the page after a dedication or epigraph still opens on a right-hand page unless it too says it starts on the next one.
- **Where the choice is offered:** `layoutBook` also reported `canFollowOn` per entry: the rules would open that entry a page later, so the choice had a blank page to remove. The row menu disabled the checkbox and said "This page already starts on the next page." when it was false. Front matter often has no blank to remove (the first page, a preface a few pages in, two pages the rules already put flush), and a tick that changed nothing there read as a broken setting. Both are gone: the menu offers three starts instead, see "Page start: three choices" below.
- **Checked against real Typst:** `packages/export-pdf/src/__tests__/pagenumbers.render.test.ts` renders a fixture book with the choice on and asserts that `layoutBook` gives the same start pages, printed numbers and total page count as Typst, and that the blank page before the dedication is gone while the foreword after it still opens on a right-hand page. A second case puts the choice on the first back matter page of a book without chapters, where it meets the arabic restart.

### Why

Front matter is often several short pages in a row (two quotes, a dedication and an epigraph); the printing rule then inserts blank left-hand pages between them, which read as empty pages the author cannot edit or remove. Keeping the rule, because a dedication should open on a right-hand page in print, but letting one page opt out, fixes the author's problem without a book-wide switch. One layout rule in `@cadabracraft/shared` and one in the Typst document keep the estimate and the printed book in step, which the real-Typst test enforces.

### Rejected alternatives

| Rejected                                                    | Why not                                                                                                                                                                           |
| ----------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Hide the blank page in the editor only                      | The blank is in the PDF: the page cards would disagree with the printed book, and the page numbers after it would be wrong on screen.                                             |
| Drop the right-hand-page rule for dedications and epigraphs | A dedication on a left-hand page is wrong in print; the rule should stay the default and only the exceptional page opt out.                                                       |
| A book-level "no blank pages" switch                        | Too broad: the right answer differs from page to page, and the rule exists for a reason.                                                                                          |
| Draw the blank as an editable page                          | It has no content in the PDF (the page does not exist once the rule is off), so text typed there would be lost or would silently change the pagination of everything after it.    |
| Force the restart page onto a right-hand page regardless    | The author asked for that page to follow on; a page that both restarts the count and starts on the next page keeps its choice, and both the layout and the PDF do the same thing. |
| No version bump                                             | An older app would drop the setting on its next save.                                                                                                                             |

### Follow-ups

- The pen does not draw the row menu group: the app adds a "Page start" radio group (see "Page start: three choices") after the matter page's groups, as it did for the vertical position group. Roadmap line added.
- A blank page the book inserts (for a right-hand start) is still not a page the author can select or annotate; the "Page start" choice is the way to add or remove one. A blank page the author adds is a page of its own, see "Blank pages the author adds".

## Page start: three choices (2026-11-04)

### Decision

The row menu's single "Start on the next page" checkbox is a three-way **Page start** radio group, laid out like the "Vertical position" group above it: **Follows the book** (no `startOnNext`), **Start on the next page** (`true`) and **Start on a right-hand page** (`false`). The first two were the old tick and its unticked state. The third is new: it inserts the blank left-hand page that puts the page on a right-hand one, on a page the rules would otherwise leave on a left-hand page (a disclaimer, a quote page, a preface of a template that starts chapters on any page).

- **One layout rule:** `layoutBook` in `packages/shared/src/pageNumbering.ts` treats `startOnNext === false` as a right-hand start whatever the rules and the template say, and `true` still skips every right-hand push, the arabic restart's included. `PlacedEntry.canFollowOn` is gone with the disabled state and the "This page already starts on the next page." note it fed: the group is always offered, because a page can be given a blank page as well as have one taken away.
- **PDF (Typst):** `bodyOf` in `packages/export-pdf/src/typst/matter.ts` breaks `to: "odd"` before a page whose choice is `false`, the page that restarts the arabic count included, and passes `odd: false` to `#chapter-start` only for `true`. The weak break is enough for the wrappers that lay out a page by kind (`centered-page`, `copyright-page`), which start nothing new of their own.
- **The menu:** `EntryList.tsx` renders a Radix `RadioGroup` with the label "Page start"; choosing writes `setMatterStartOnNext(m, id, true | false | null)`, so the three choices go through book undo and redo like any other structure change, and the live region announces the result ("Preface: starts on a right-hand page"). Chapters still have no such group.
- **Checked against real Typst:** the shared layout's start pages, printed folios and page total for `startOnNext: false` are compared with a real Typst render of the fixture book (Modern Memoir, which starts chapters on any page, so the preface has a blank page to gain) in `packages/export-pdf/src/__tests__/pagenumbers.render.test.ts`; `matter.test.ts` checks the emitted break for both a page using the chapter opening and a centred one, and `matter-start.spec.ts` drives the group in the app and counts the page cards.

### Why

The old checkbox could only take a blank page away. On the two pages whose author asked about it — a disclaimer and a quote page that had been set to follow on earlier — the tick was already on and greyed out, with a note saying there was nothing to change there, and the author who wanted the blank page back had no way to ask for it. Naming the three starts says what the page does, instead of leaving the author to work the book's printing rules out from the page cards.

### Rejected alternatives

| Rejected                                                 | Why not                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| -------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Make the tick insert a blank page when it is on          | It already means the opposite everywhere else ("follows on, left-hand page or not") and that is the behaviour authors ticked it for; reusing it would turn their pages around.                                                                 |
| Keep the checkbox and hide it where it changes nothing   | That was the reported bug: the pages that need a blank page inserted are exactly the ones the old menu greyed out.                                                                                                                             |
| Offer only "Start on a right-hand page" and "Follows on" | The book's rules are the right answer most of the time (a dedication opens on a right-hand page, a chapter follows the template); an author should not have to restate them to keep them.                                                      |
| Move the arabic restart with the choice                  | A page that both restarts the count and starts on the next page keeps its choice, keeps 1 on a left-hand page, and the layout and the PDF agree (see "Pages that start on the next page").                                                     |
| `formatVersion` 11                                       | Nothing in the file changes shape: `startOnNext` is still a boolean, `false` was already valid and an older app keeps it on its next save. It draws that page without the blank, which is what that app did before, and no setting is dropped. |

### Follow-ups

- The pen does not draw the group: the app adds the "Page start" radio group after the matter page's other groups, as it does for "Vertical position". Roadmap line added.
- The three labels sit close to one another in meaning. If authors still ask for "start on the next page" to mean "leave a blank page", the labels ("Follows the book" / "Start on the next page" / "Start on a right-hand page") are where to say so more plainly.

## Scrolling between sections in Write mode (2026-11-03)

### Decision

Write mode scrolls continuously through the book. At the last page of the open section (a front matter page, a chapter or a back matter page) more scrolling down opens the next section at its first page; above the first page, scrolling up opens the previous one and lands on its last page, so the two sections read as one document. Sections stay separate files with their own editors and page stacks; only the handover is new. A phone's reflowed page does the same.

- **`lib/reading/sectionScroll.ts` (pure):** `edgeStep` turns a wheel event into "still inside" or "the next/previous section". Travel past an edge accumulates in CSS px (`EDGE_TRAVEL` 100; lines and pages are converted) so a whole wheel notch hands over and a trackpad tick does not; turning round, leaving the edge or scrolling in the middle starts the count again. A scroller that fits its content is at both ends at once, so a one-page section hands over too. `sectionNeighbours` reads the neighbours from `readingOrder`; `requestLanding` / `takeLanding` carry where the next section's scroller should land (its start when coming from above, its end when coming from below) because the frame unmounts and mounts again.
- **`editor/useSectionScroll.ts`:** the wheel handler for the page stack's scroller and the frame's `onWheel`. It opens the neighbour through `actions.selectChapter` inside `attempt`, so a failed open is reported like any other, and from below it follows the bottom of the new stack while the pagination settles (`Typst`'s breaks arrive after the estimate). The author scrolling themselves cancels a pending landing so the page never jumps under them.
- **Where:** `PageFrame` (page view, every desktop and tablet width) and `ReflowFrame` (the phone's plain reading text). Read mode and the read-only previews are untouched: they already scroll the whole book.

### Why

A chapter usually ends in the middle of the pane, and the author had to go back to the sidebar for every front matter page. Continuing the scroll is what a book-shaped surface is expected to do, and it needed no change to how pages are laid out, saved or measured.

### Rejected alternatives

| Rejected                                  | Why not                                                                                                                                              |
| ----------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| One editor for the whole book             | Rewrites saving, undo, find and pagination at once; the per-page files exist so a page can be saved, backed up and synced on its own.                |
| Hand over on any wheel event at the edge  | A trackpad tick at the end of a page would skip a whole chapter; the travel threshold keeps the gesture deliberate.                                  |
| Wrap around at the first and last section | The start and the end of the book should stay put, so the author cannot lose their place.                                                            |
| Scrolling the neighbour in, side by side  | Two editors and two page stacks mounted at once, with their own measuring and typewriter scrolling, for a transition the author only passes through. |

### Follow-ups

- The pen does not draw the handover (a section ending mid-pane); the frames show one section per screen. Roadmap line added.
- A fast fling that reaches the last page hands over in the same gesture, because the delta left over after the bottom counts as travel. If that feels eager in use, the fix is to count only a gesture that started at the edge.

## Text alignment (2026-11-03)

**Decision:** paragraphs and headings can be aligned left, centred or right, per block, in the editor and in every export. Justified text stays a template matter (the body text of every built-in template is justified); there is no "justify" choice.

- **Schema.** `textAlign` is a global attribute (`TextAlignment`, a `addGlobalAttributes` extension in `packages/editor-schema`) on `paragraph` and `heading` only. Values `start`, `center`, `end`; absent (or `null`) means "what the template does", which is also what every book written before this has. `chapterDocProblem` refuses any other value, so the app, the MCP server and the importers share one rule. It is stored on the paragraph inside a block quote, a list item, a task item, a definition description or a table cell, so those inherit the feature without a node type of their own: the bullet or number stays where the list puts it and the cell keeps its own per-paragraph alignment. Code blocks (a `pre` block must keep its shape), scene breaks (drawn centred by the template) and definition terms (inline only) have no attribute, and a table as a whole has none.
- **Logical, not physical.** `start` and `end` are the sides a line begins and ends on, like CSS `text-align: start|end`: a Hebrew or Arabic book mirrors with no per-book value and no render-time flipping. `left`/`right` would have to mean different things per book, because HTML `align="left"` and CSS `text-align: left` are physical (they do not mirror) while Word's `w:jc` `left`/`right` do flip in a right-to-left paragraph. Word and RTF are only written for left-to-right books today, so mapping `start`→`left`/`\ql` and `end`→`right`/`\qr` is exact there; an RTL Word export would need `w:bidi` on the paragraph as well, and is not written.
- **No first-line indent, no justification.** An aligned block is written with `text-indent: 0` and `justify: false`: justified text has no ragged side to align, and the template's indent would push a centred line off centre. An aligned first paragraph also loses the drop cap (it hangs off the left margin of justified text).
- **UI.** One "Text alignment" button in the toolbar (`AlignmentMenu.tsx`), in the block-types group after the lists, opening a radio group: Align to Start, Centre, Align to End, Template Default. It shows the alignment of the block the caret is in, so the bar says what the paragraph does. It joins the roving tab stop (arrow keys, Home, End, mirrored in RTL) before More formatting. To keep the bar on one row at 1280 px (18 buttons is its limit, see "Toolbar overflow menu"), Scene break moved from the bar into the More formatting menu, where Image details already is; it keeps its Format menu item and its id. The same four commands are in Format > Alignment (ticked checkbox items, because the native menu API has no radio items; the app unticks the other three after each choice) and in the command palette by itself (built from the menu model). Keys `Mod+Shift+L` / `Mod+Shift+C` / `Mod+Shift+R`; `Mod+Shift+E`, the obvious key for "centre", is Print PDF, and a menu accelerator wins the key before the editor sees it. Template Default has no key. The labels are logical ("Align to Start", not "Align Left") so an Arabic or Hebrew interface does not say "left" for a line that is aligned right; the start and end icons mirror in RTL (`ui/directional.ts`). Enter keeps the alignment on the next block, like a word processor.
- **On disk.** `chapterJson(editor)` writes the editor's JSON without `textAlign: null` (and without empty `attrs`), so a paragraph that follows the template is exactly the JSON it was before and only an aligned block gains `"attrs": {"textAlign": "..."}`.
- **Exports.** PDF: a scope `#[#set align(...) #set par(first-line-indent: 0pt, justify: false)]`; a heading says what it wants through its `supplement` (`[ha-center]`, read by `heading-align` in the preamble), because the template's `#show heading` rule sets the alignment inside its own block. EPUB: `class="align-start|center|end"` on the `<p>`/`<h1..3>` and three rules appended to the stylesheet (after a template's own, so they win at equal specificity), which also switch the drop cap off for an aligned opening paragraph; no inline styles, so the text still reflows. DOCX: `w:jc` (`left`/`center`/`right`) in the paragraph's `w:pPr`, with the first-line indent zeroed. RTF: `\ql`/`\qc`/`\qr` with `\fi0`. Markdown has no syntax for it: with `htmlMarks` a top-level block is wrapped in `<div align="...">` with blank lines around it (inside a list item, quote or cell a `<div>` would change what those mean, so it is left plain there); without `htmlMarks` the alignment is lost, like underline, small caps, superscript and subscript. Importers read it back from `data-text-align`, `align="..."`, `style="text-align: ..."`, `<center>`, a class (`align-center`, `text-center`), DOCX `w:jc` (centre and right only: left and justified are what style generators write on every paragraph, so reading them would make a whole book ragged) and the Markdown wrapper. MCP: the JSON read and write tools carry the attribute untouched, and `cadabra_export_markdown` documents the wrapper.
- **Views.** `index.css` styles `[data-text-align]` for the editor, Page view and Read mode, and the paginator measures the real CSS, so a page break follows an aligned paragraph without a second code path. The Contents page is generated from the outline, so it is not affected. The audiobook reads text only, so it is not affected either.
- **Version.** No `formatVersion` bump: chapter files are never migrated, so a bump would lock older apps out of whole books without touching the text (see "Strike, inline code, superscript and subscript"). Caveat: an older app drops an attribute it does not know when it next saves that chapter, so alignment written by this version is lost if an older build edits and saves the same chapter. The schema (app and MCP) refuses a chapter with an unknown value, so a newer file is never silently reinterpreted. `docs/file-format.md` documents the attribute.

**Why:** alignment is the one piece of block formatting authors ask for that was missing, and every format we write has a native way to express it, so the contract stays in one place (the schema) and no exporter guesses. A global attribute instead of four node types keeps the chapter shape, the paginator and the contents list unchanged. Logical values keep right-to-left books correct without a second attribute or a per-book setting.

**Rejected:**

| Rejected                                      | Why not                                                                                                                                                                               |
| --------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `@tiptap/extension-text-align`                | A new dependency for the same twenty lines; it writes `style="text-align: ..."`, an inline style we do not want in the EPUB, and it configures the same node list anyway.             |
| Physical `left` / `right` values              | CSS and HTML do not mirror them while Word does, so one stored value would render differently per format in an RTL book; logical values say one thing and every format maps it.       |
| A "justify" choice                            | Ragged or justified text is the book's look (the template), not a per-paragraph decision; offering it would let an author mix both in one chapter.                                    |
| A node or mark per alignment                  | Changes the shape of every chapter and every exporter for something that belongs to a block property.                                                                                 |
| Inline styles in the EPUB                     | The reading system's own indent and margins then lose to ours, and the stylesheet can no longer be themed; a class keeps the reflow clean.                                            |
| Three separate toolbar buttons                | The bar must stay on one row at 1280 px (see "Toolbar overflow menu: More formatting") and it holds 18 buttons; three more would wrap it and shrink the writing area on every screen. |
| A second row for the alignment buttons        | Two rows cost writing space permanently, and the project already rejected that for the language button ("Toolbar overflow menu").                                                     |
| A second `formatVersion` for the attribute    | Migrations only rewrite the manifest, so a bump protects nothing in the chapter files while making every aligned book unopenable in an older build.                                   |
| Reading `w:jc="left"` back as start in DOCX   | Word writes it on every paragraph of a generated manuscript, so every paragraph of an imported book would be pinned to start and no longer follow the template.                       |
| Wrapping every Markdown block, lists included | `<div>` inside a list item or a table cell changes what the Markdown means, and CommonMark does not allow it there; plain text is the honest lossy output.                            |

**Follow-ups / caveats:** pen frames for the toolbar button and its menu, the Format > Alignment submenu (ticked and unticked) and the RTL mirrored button are not drawn (the pen is not changed by this work; a roadmap line was added). Word and RTF map logical start/end to left/right, so they are only right for left-to-right books. The EPUB rules are appended after a template stylesheet at equal specificity, so a template that styles `p` with a more specific selector (an id or `!important`) would still win. Aligning a whole list means selecting its items; there is no "align the list" command.

## Page numbering (2026-11-03)

### Decision

One pure function owns the page numbers of the book: `layoutBook(entries, { startsOn, numbered })` in `packages/shared/src/pageNumbering.ts`. It takes the book's reading order, the template's chapter opening and one page count per entry, and returns for every entry the physical page it starts on (blank verso included), whether a blank verso precedes it, its last page, its numbering style (`roman`/`arabic`) and the number printed on its first page, plus the book's physical length, where arabic 1 is and whether every count was measured. Every surface that names a page — Write page cards, the status bar, Read mode, the print preview, the Contents page and the checks in the PDF dialog — calls it (directly or through the two desktop adapters below) and nothing else.

**The rules are the PDF's** (`packages/export-pdf/src/typst/matter.ts`, which `pagenumbers.render.test.ts` checks against real Typst for three templates):

- Physical pages count from 1, the cover not counted (the measure runs without a cover, so the report's pages are interior pages).
- Front matter is numbered in lower-case roman by physical page. The title, copyright and contents pages print no folio; every other front matter page prints its roman number.
- The first page after the front matter opens on a right-hand (odd) page and restarts at arabic 1; every later page, back matter and blank versos included, continues that count. A book with no front matter is arabic from page 1.
- Title page, contents, dedication and epigraph always open on a right-hand page, and so does the page after a dedication or epigraph. A chapter, and a front or back matter page that is not laid out in the vertical middle, opens like the template says (`chapterOpening.startOn`: `recto` puts a blank verso first when the next page would be even). Every other entry opens on the next page.
- A blank verso counts in the page numbers and prints its folio, and has no running head; it is never a page entry of its own.
- When Typst's report is of the text as it is now and has every entry, its `start` pages win over the rules: the layout then _is_ the PDF, blank pages and all.

**Where the numbers come from**, best first: Typst's report for the current text (`measurePages`, `PageReport.starts`); the page count an editor laid out for an entry (a blank page before it not counted, `state/pages/pageCount.ts`'s `byId`); Typst's page count from an older report (a chapter's start may move, its length rarely does); else `ceil(words / words per page)` from the book's template and trim, at least one page. Each entry says whether its count is `exact`; the book is exact when all are. The words come from `entryWords()` in the project store, which counts every page in reading order including front and back matter and gives an entry with no file in the open language 0 words, so an untranslated page still takes its page.

**The adapters**, so no surface does its own arithmetic:

- `apps/desktop/src/lib/page/pageNumbering.ts`: `bookLayout(book, entries, sources)` turns the template (trim, margins, font, `startOn`) and `PageSources` (report, `fresh`, measured map, `numbered`) into `layoutBook` inputs. `chapterStart`, `bookWordsPerPage` and `chapterPageCount` stay here as the estimate's inputs.
- `apps/desktop/src/lib/page/bookPages.ts`: `layoutOfPages(book, pages, sources)` does the same from a `BookPage[]` (Read mode, the print preview and the PDF panel read the book that way), and `bookPages()` is the summary the print checks need (`trimSize`, `pages` = `physical`, `exact`).
- `apps/desktop/src/app/hooks/useBookLayout.ts` is the single hook Write mode, the status bar and the Contents editor mount: it refreshes the word count when the book changes (`state/bookWords.ts`), reads the report (`state/pages/pageMeasure.ts`) and the editors' measures (`state/pages/pageCount.ts`), and returns the `BookPages`. The store is keyed by template/trim/font so a measure under another layout is dropped.

**How each surface consumes it:**

| Surface                           | Reads                                                                                                | Shows                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| --------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Write page cards                  | the open entry's `cardStart` as the pagination's `firstPage`, `folioText(layout, physical)` per card | physical page numbers on blank cards, roman on front matter, nothing on title/copyright/contents, arabic from chapter 1 on; running heads by physical parity                                                                                                  |
| Write status bar (`Page n of m`)  | the cursor card's physical page → `folioAt`, `layout.physical`                                       | the book's number of the page under the cursor and the book's last page; `≈` before the number only when an entry before it is estimated (so an entry's own unmeasured length never marks its first page), `≈` before the total when any count is an estimate |
| Write status bar (`PageEstimate`) | `layout.physical`, `layout.exact`                                                                    | " · 174 pages at 6 × 9 in", `≈` while estimated: total including front and back matter and blank versos                                                                                                                                                       |
| Read mode                         | the same layout, from the pages it loaded                                                            | the same cards and folios as Write, chained across every section; the footer's page is `folioAt` of the card at the reading line                                                                                                                              |
| Print preview                     | the real Typst PDF (`TypstSpread`); the layout only for the fallback count and the "About N pages"   | the PDF's own folios; the fallback counts `physical`, `≈` while estimated                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| PDF dialog checks                 | `bookPages()`                                                                                        | `physical` pages for the print checks                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| Contents page                     | the layout per entry (report when fresh; estimate marked `≈`)                                        | roman on front matter, nothing for title/copyright/contents ("–"), arabic for chapters and back matter, exactly the numbers the PDF's `toc-list` prints                                                                                                       |
| EPUB                              | —                                                                                                    | no page list or pagebreak markers are written; reflowable, so there is nothing to number                                                                                                                                                                      |

In Read mode every section's page count goes back into the same store (`setEntryPages`), so the reader's chain is Write mode's, not a chain of its own; the reader's "scroll to the open chapter" effects read `align`/`measure` through refs and depend only on the open chapter, because a page count arriving makes those functions new and re-running a jump would scroll the reader back to the chapter they came from.

**While measurement is pending** the layout is built from estimates and marked `≈`; it is never a structurally wrong 1. Front matter is still roman (the title page is `i`, the contents page `iii`, not `1`), and chapter 2 still starts after chapter 1's estimated pages. A floored page count of one page per empty entry keeps the chain from collapsing. The surfaces keep the last numbers while a new count arrives (they never flash a placeholder), and a stale report may still supply page _counts_ (but not starts).

**Editions.** The layout is per edition: it counts the pages the open edition has. An entry with no file in that language is an empty page in the layout (so chapter 3 of a partly translated edition starts after the two empty chapters before it, as the export's PDF would lay them out). `countEntries` no longer skips those entries. An export with `skipUntranslated` leaves them out, and that export's own Typst report is its truth.

**Hidden and untranslated Contents entries** do not change pagination (hiding is a list setting, not a layout one). A custom Contents entry shows its target's number. Heading numbers still come from the report's `h` marks (`printedNumber`), which is also the only source that can be exact for a heading.

**RTL books** use the same numbers: recto is still the odd physical page (Typst's `pagebreak(to: "odd")` is physical), the binding side only moves the margins (`sideMargins`) and the running heads. Roman numerals are written in the same order (they are not mirrored in the PDF either).

**`numbered: false`** (the PDF dialog's switch) gives every entry `number: null`: the layout still places pages and reports lengths, but no folio is drawn anywhere.

### Why

The app had four page-number chains: chapters chained from their word counts in Write (`firstPageNumber`), from measured card counts in Read (`chainFirstPages`), from the Typst report per chapter, and front and back matter restarted at 1 in every one of them; the totals (`bookPageCount`) counted chapters only. Each was right about a different part and they disagreed about front matter, blank versos, estimates and failed counts, so the same book showed `1` on its title page, `1` on its acknowledgments, `2 of 6` on every chapter in the status bar and a total that left the front matter out. One function with the PDF's rules and one set of inputs makes every surface agree by construction, and the render test fixes the rules to Typst's rather than to our reading of them. It also removes the estimate's missing blank verso, which made chapter starts jump by a page when Typst's report arrived (estimate 6, PDF 7).

### Rejected alternatives

| Rejected                                                                  | Why not                                                                                                                                                                     |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Keep a per-surface chain and align them by hand                           | What the bug was: four implementations, three answers. Any new surface restarts the same drift.                                                                             |
| Compile each chapter with Typst and read its first page number            | A per-chapter compile resets `counter(page)`; making it not reset means compiling the whole book anyway, and it would need Typst for a number the app must show without it. |
| Number front matter in arabic (physical page)                             | The PDF prints roman (and none on the title, copyright and contents pages); the editor would disagree with the export it is previewing.                                     |
| Number each entry from its own page 1 and show only the chapter's numbers | That is the current bug in the status bar; an author needs the printed page to find it in the PDF or a proof.                                                               |
| A mutable "next page" cursor shared through the store                     | Order-dependent and untestable; a pure function over the reading order gives every surface the same answer and is unit-testable at 100%.                                    |
| Count pages from words only                                               | Exact page counts exist (Typst's report, the editor's own layout); throwing them away makes the numbers jump when the report lands.                                         |
| Start the estimate at 0 and let each surface add one                      | It hides the off-by-one in each caller instead of in one place; the first arabic page is a rule of the layout.                                                              |
| Re-measure the whole book on every edit so numbers are never estimates    | Seconds per measure on a large book; the estimate with `≈` is the honest answer in between.                                                                                 |

### Test matrix

`pagenumbers.render.test.ts` (real Typst, three templates) pins the rules to the PDF. `packages/shared` unit tests cover every rule and branch at 100%. The Playwright spec `page-numbers.spec.ts` uses one book — title page, copyright, contents, dedication, three chapters of several pages and two back matter pages — and asserts:

| Situation                     | Write cards                               | Status bar         | Read cards             | Contents                     | Preview / checks                              |
| ----------------------------- | ----------------------------------------- | ------------------ | ---------------------- | ---------------------------- | --------------------------------------------- |
| Estimates only (no Typst)     | i / – / – / v / 1, 7, 13, back matter 19  | `Page 7 of ≈ 24`   | same as Write, chained | roman + arabic, `≈`          | `About 24 pages`, `≈`                         |
| Typst report current          | the report's pages, blank versos included | exact, no `≈`      | same                   | the report's numbers, no `≈` | real PDF counts, no `≈`                       |
| Book with no front matter     | chapter 1 = 1                             | `Page 1 of 17`     | same                   | arabic only                  | —                                             |
| `startsOn: "any"` template    | no blank versos, numbers consecutive      | —                  | same                   | —                            | —                                             |
| Page numbers off (PDF switch) | no folios                                 | —                  | no folios              | "–"                          | PDF prints none                               |
| Page view off / phone width   | reflow (no cards)                         | the book's numbers | plain reading          | numbers still right          | —                                             |
| Untranslated earlier chapter  | the later chapter keeps its slot          | —                  | same                   | same                         | `skipUntranslated` export uses its own report |
| RTL edition (he/ar)           | same numbers, mirrored margins            | —                  | same                   | same                         | —                                             |

### Quality gates

| Gate                 | Applies                 | Command / rule                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| -------------------- | ----------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Feature completeness | Yes                     | Every surface above reads `layoutBook`; `docs/architecture.md` (this section), README/`docs/testing.md` notes updated. No pen change: no new screen, control or state (folios and status-bar text change, layout does not). |
| Typecheck            | Yes                     | `pnpm typecheck`                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| Lint and format      | Yes                     | `pnpm lint`, `pnpm format:check`                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| i18n                 | Yes if a string changes | `pnpm --filter @cadabracraft/desktop i18n:extract`, fill en/es/it/fr/de/he/ar to 0 missing, `i18n:check` after staging                                                                                                      |
| ADA (WCAG 2.2 AA)    | Only text changes       | `/a11y --fast`; no new screen, so no new entry in `a11yScreens.ts`; page cards stay `aria-hidden`, the status bar stays a live text node                                                                                    |
| Test coverage        | Yes                     | `packages/shared` 100%; desktop unit floors (lines/statements/functions/branches) 99/99/99/98 and the page-view/reading/toc/export specs in Chromium with a unique `E2E_PORT`                                               |
| EPUB                 | Not changed             | `/validate-epub` not required (no exporter touched); run it anyway if `export-epub` changes                                                                                                                                 |

### Follow-ups / caveats

- The estimate counts a contents page as its title's words, so without Typst a long contents list can add a page the estimate does not know about; the report corrects it, and the estimate is marked `≈`.
- The status bar total is the interior page count (no cover). With a cover the printed PDF has two more pages before arabic 1, as the export dialog already says.
- Real-Typst checks need the `typst` binary; the render tests skip without it and say so (`REQUIRE_TYPST=1` makes that a failure in CI).
- Word, RTF and Markdown exports have no pages and are unaffected.

## PDF fidelity: blank lines, the drop cap, the chapter opening (2026-11-04)

### Decision

Three things the Typst PDF set differently from the page the author sees in the app (Page view, Read mode) and from the EPUB. The report was "the layout and spacing isn't preserved" in the exported PDF; a page-by-page comparison of one real book (eight front-matter pages, Modern Memoir, 6 × 9 in with 0.125 in bleed, 14 pt/10.5 pt body) found these three, and nothing else: with them fixed, every page's text sits where the page view draws it, to within a line's wrap.

- **A paragraph the author left empty is one line of the body.** The book separates its paragraphs with an empty paragraph (Enter twice), and Page view and the EPUB give it the height of a line, but `block()` in `blocks.ts` skipped empty paragraphs, so every page after a blank line ran short and the exported book was two pages shorter than the preview. `isBlank(kids)` (no text at all) now writes `#blank-line()`: `context v(measure([a\ b]).height - measure([a]).height)`, which is exactly one line at whatever font, size and leading it sits in, so an empty paragraph inside a quote or a footnote is one of _those_ lines; the paragraph spacing on either side is unchanged (a `v()` is no block, so the neighbours' spacing still collapses as before). A blank line is no leaf: it carries no page or word mark (there is no text to find), which keeps the exact-breaks report and `exactBreaks` as they were.
- **The drop cap hangs under the chapter's own title.** `dropCapOpening` (in `matter.ts`) looked at `doc[0]` only, so a chapter that opens with its H1 — what "Add chapter" writes, and what Page view and the EPUB cap (`h1:first-child + p`) — got no drop cap at all, and one whose H1 was followed by a blank line got the cap on the blank line and lost it. It now takes the first paragraph under a leading level-1 heading, sets that heading above the cap with block 0's marks, and gives an indenting template's `#h()` to the first paragraph that has text, so a blank line the author left under the title keeps its place.
- **No chapter opening above a page that has no title.** `#chapter-start` applied the template's opening — the sink, the ornament and the label — to every front or back matter page that was not centred, so a matter page whose title is a heading 2 (`IMPORTANT DISCLAIMER`) began one sink (6 em, 63 pt at 10.5 pt) below the top margin, and its whole text with it. `chapter-start` takes `opening: true` (the default, so every other page is generated as before) and `bodyOf` passes `opening: false` when the page's first block is not a level-1 heading — the rule Page view already applies through `h1:first-child`, and the EPUB by having no opening at all. Chapters keep their opening whatever their first block, because a chapter's label and ornament are the template's, not the document's.

### Why

The page view is a preview of the printed page: an author sets the page they can see, and an export that drops their blank lines, leaves the ornament off the cap or pushes a whole page down is wrong even though each rule was internally consistent. All three were the PDF's own bugs, not a choice the other surfaces had made differently, so they are fixed in the PDF rather than by changing what the app draws or what the EPUB writes.

### Rejected alternatives

| Rejected                                                                 | Why not                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Keep skipping empty paragraphs (a blank line is not content)             | The author writes with them, Page view and the EPUB print them, and the PDF then paginates differently from both; a print book has to set the page the author wrote.                                                   |
| Drop empty paragraphs in Page view and the EPUB instead                  | Thousands of existing books would change on screen and their page counts with them, and the author's spacing would be silently removed everywhere.                                                                     |
| A fixed `v(1em)` for the blank line                                      | A line's height follows the leading (1.47 em in most templates, more inside a quote at a larger size); `1em` is about a third of a line short and wrong wherever the local size differs.                               |
| Leave the cap on `doc[0]` and require the chapter to start with text     | A chapter opened from the app begins with its title, so it would never get the letter the template promises; Page view and the EPUB already put it under that title.                                                   |
| Take the cap on the paragraph under any leading heading                  | A heading 2 opens a section, not the chapter; Page view's `h1 + p` and the EPUB's `::first-letter` rule both say so.                                                                                                   |
| Drop the opening for every matter page ("matter has no chapter opening") | A Preface or Foreword that opens with its own title keeps the sink in Page view, and the opening is what the template's `chapterOpening` data is for; only a page without a level-1 heading has nothing to hang it on. |
| Count the sink in Page view for matter pages too                         | It would push those pages down in the preview to match a PDF rule the EPUB (and the template's own intent, a sink _above a title_) does not have; the PDF is the outlier here.                                         |

### Follow-ups / caveats

- Of the three print layouts Page view drew plainly, two are drawn as the PDF sets them since 2026-11-04 (see "Page view draws the copyright imprint and a centred page's block" below): the copyright page's imprint at the foot in 0.85 em with 0.7 em spacing, and a centred matter page's 78 % block with centred lines. The third — a running head on a matter page's continuation pages — is drawn since the same day too (see "Running heads on matter pages' continuation pages" below), so all three now match the PDF.
- `fixtures/golden/sample-chapter.typ` is regenerated (`UPDATE_GOLDEN=1`), because the preamble changed; the diff is the `blank-line` helper and the `opening` parameter.
- Checked with the real Typst 0.15.1 binary: `blankline.render.test.ts` (a blank line is exactly one line pitch, and keeps its place under a title and a cap) and the rest of the render suite; `typst.test.ts` covers the source for all three.

## Page view draws the copyright imprint and a centred page's block (2026-11-04)

### Decision

The last two print layouts Page view drew plainly are now the PDF's: the copyright page sets its printer's imprint at the foot of the text box, and a centred matter page sets its text in a block 78 % of the text width with its lines centred. Read mode draws them too, because it shares the page stack; the phone's plain reading layout and the EPUB are unchanged.
