**Trade-off:** an older app that opens and saves a book with `sceneBreak` drops the key (as it does for `trimSize` and `fonts`), and the book falls back to the template's glyph. Reverting that needs a version bump, which is the alternative above.

**Follow-ups / caveats:** the Page view (`apps/desktop/src/lib/page/pageLayout.ts`) still draws the template's glyph, not the chosen one, until the picker lands with the pen designs. A `rule` ornament (a line instead of a glyph) is not in the library: the modern template's EPUB draws a border and its PDF an em dash, so a rule needs its own layout code.

## Read aloud in the editor: one sample call per sentence, played in the webview (2026-11-09)

**Decision:** "Read aloud" (`apps/desktop/src/editor/readAloud/`) reads the open chapter with the same bundled local voice as the audiobook and "Hear a sample". Each sentence is one `audio_sample` call (the existing WAV-bytes command, one chunk, the Settings voice or the best installed one for the language, the bar's speed). The WAV is played from a Blob URL in an `HTMLAudioElement`, and the next sentence is made while the current one plays. The position is a pure state machine (`machine.ts`), the sentences are read from the ProseMirror document (`sentenceList.ts`), and the highlight is a ProseMirror decoration (`highlight.ts`), so the text never changes. Any document update stops reading.

**Why:** the engine already returns bytes for a clip and the webview already plays them (the sample player), so no new Rust command, no file in the project and no new dependency are needed. Clips are never written to the project, which keeps the file format unchanged.

Rejected:

| Option                                                    | Why not                                                                                                                                                               |
| --------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| One `audio_render_chapter` call for the whole chapter     | It writes files into the project and renders the chapter before the first word, so the reader would wait for minutes and get no sentence timing.                      |
| A new Rust command that streams sentences over a channel  | More code on the Rust side for the same bytes; the sample command already does what is needed.                                                                        |
| Web Speech API (`speechSynthesis`)                        | Uses the operating system's voices, not the bundled local engine, so the voice would differ from the audiobook and proofing would not hear what the book sounds like. |
| Highlight by wrapping each sentence in a DOM span or mark | Changes the document, so the stored text would change and the history would fill up. A decoration changes nothing on disk.                                            |

**Trade-off:** each sentence starts the engine once, so the first sentence waits for its clip and a slow voice can lag the reading. The next clip is prepared early to hide most of this. Speed changes restart the reading from the sentence being read.

**Follow-ups / caveats:** sentences are split on `. ! ?` and the ellipsis, and a sentence over 300 characters is cut at a space for the engine, so the highlight covers that piece. Footnote text is not read (footnotes are inline nodes). The bar sits under the toolbar in every view, including Focus mode.

## A persisted book goal and its daily plan (2026-11-09, format 13)

**Decision:** the writing goal is the optional `goal` object in `cadabra.json`: `words` (a whole number from 1 to 10,000,000) and `deadline` (optional, a real `YYYY-MM-DD` date). It is a format bump to 13. The migration from 12 changes nothing (absent means no goal), and a test opens a real v12 manifest and checks that every entry, title, file, reference and edition survives. `dailyPlan(goal, bookWords, today)` in `packages/format/src/book/goal.ts` is pure: it returns what remains, whether the goal is met, the days left to the deadline (today counts as one day, so a deadline of today leaves one), the words per day needed (rounded up), and whether the deadline passed unmet. Only the goal is stored; the plan is derived and never written. The date helpers (`isDateKey`, `daysBetween`, `shiftDateKey`, `weekdayOf`) live in `packages/shared/src/dates.ts` and do whole-day UTC arithmetic, so a daylight-saving change cannot move a deadline. The font validator moved to `packages/format/src/book/fonts.ts` so that `manifest.ts` stays under the 400-line limit.

**Why:** a goal describes one manuscript. In the manifest it travels with the folder, syncs with it, and is seen by the headless MCP tools. The version is bumped because an older app that loads and saves the manifest would drop the key, the same reason `publishing` bumped the version (formatVersion 6).

| Rejected                                             | Why                                                                                                                                        |
| ---------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Keep the goal in the app's preferences (per machine) | A goal belongs to the book. Per-app preferences would give every book the same target and would not follow the folder to another computer. |
| Store the daily plan on disk next to the goal        | The plan changes with every word typed; a stored copy would go stale. It is derived from the goal, the book's words and today's date.      |
| Add the field without a version bump                 | An older app would drop `goal` on its next save, silently losing the author's target. The bump makes an older app refuse the book instead. |
| Take "today" from the clock inside `dailyPlan`       | Makes the function impure and its tests depend on the date. The caller passes the local date as `YYYY-MM-DD`.                              |

**Caveats:** the format package's strict 100% coverage gate is not met at this commit. The gaps are in code this change did not touch (`edit/chapters.ts`, `edit/matter.ts`, `toc/edit.ts`, and one `toc.parts` type check in `manifest.ts`), so they were already there before this bump. The new modules are fully covered.

## Writing history in `stats.json`, counted at each save (2026-11-09, no format change)

**Decision:** the words written per day are one file in the project folder, `stats.json` (see [file-format.md](file-format.md), "Writing history"). The figures and the heatmap are pure functions in `packages/shared/src/history/` (`summary.ts`, `heatmap.ts`, `file.ts`). The app counts the book's words after each page save (`countBook`, the chapters' words) and adds the difference from the last saved total to today (`apps/desktop/src/state/project/writingSave.ts`). The first count is taken when the book opens. The file is read and written through the same commands as the chapters, so the temp file, rename and hash check are the ones the chapters use. The heatmap uses a fixed scale (none, 1 to 250, 251 to 500, 501 to 1000, more than 1000 words), so one square means the same count in every book.

**Why:** the history belongs to the folder it counts, so it moves with the book and a second book keeps its own. A separate file can be deleted or lost without touching the manuscript, and it needs no format bump, because nothing in the book refers to it.

| Rejected                                                      | Why not                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| ------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Keep the history in the app's preferences                     | Preferences are per machine and per app, not per book. Two books would share one count, and the history would not follow the folder to another computer.                                  |
| Keep the history in the manifest (`cadabra.json`)             | The manifest is the book's structure and is read by the MCP tools and the exporters. Writing it at every save would make every save a manifest change, with its backup and conflict path. |
| Bump the format version and store the history in the manifest | It would change the format on every save, not once: an older app would refuse or drop the history each time it saved a book that had one. A separate file avoids both.                    |
| Count words from the editor's transactions as they are typed  | It would need a new editor hook (the editor is not changed in this milestone) and would count typing that is later undone. The save-time difference is exact and needs no editor change.  |

**Trade-offs and caveats:** the count is per save, so words typed and never saved are not counted (the autosave runs within seconds, so this is small). A change made to the book outside the app (another computer, a script) since the last save is counted at the next save in the app. A stats file that cannot be read is kept as a copy and the history starts again from empty; the app says where the copy is. The file can be deleted at any time. It is a convenience figure, not a record, so it is not part of any export.

## EPUBCheck ships with the app

**Decision (2026-11-10):** the Export dialog can validate the exported EPUB with EPUBCheck 5.4.0 (the W3C reference validator, a Java program). EPUBCheck and a trimmed Java runtime ship inside the installer as resources next to the executable, `epubcheck/` (the official jar and its `lib/`, 35 MB) and `jre/<cpu>/` (a `jlink` runtime of Temurin 17 with `java.base,java.xml,java.desktop,java.sql,java.logging,java.naming,java.security.jgss,java.compiler,jdk.unsupported,jdk.charsets,jdk.localedata`, 57 MB for one CPU). A universal macOS app carries `aarch64` and `x86_64` and the Rust side picks by `std::env::consts::ARCH`; Linux carries `x86_64`. They are staged by `scripts/stage-epubcheck.sh` and `scripts/build-jre.sh` into the gitignored `src-tauri/binaries/` (`build.rs` creates the two folders when they are missing so a checkout that did not run the scripts still builds; validating then says the checker is missing).

The Rust module `src-tauri/src/epubcheck/` mirrors `typst/`: `epubcheck_find`, `epubcheck_validate({ root, path })` and `epubcheck_cancel`. The path must be a regular file inside the book folder (the same rules as every project command), and the checker runs through `platform::sidecar::run` as `java -Xmx512m -Djava.io.tmpdir=<scratch> -jar epubcheck.jar <epub> --json <scratch>/report.json -q` with a 120 s timeout and a cancel flag. EPUBCheck exits with a failure status when the book has errors, so the JSON report decides, not the exit status. The report is cut (200 messages, 400 characters each) and returned as plain data: `{ version, counts, messages: [{ severity, id, message, file, line, column }], omitted }`. Errors are `{ kind, ... }` codes (`not_found`, `timeout`, `cancelled`, `no_epub`, `failed`, `other`) and the UI writes the words. Release builds run only the bundled copy; debug builds also honour `EPUBCHECK_DIR`, `JAVA_BIN`, `src-tauri/binaries` and a `java` on PATH. Nothing is downloaded or sent anywhere.

The UI is the EPUB panel of the Export dialog (`app/export/EpubValidation.tsx`): a **Check EPUB** button (with Stop), the result as the same toned `CheckList` rows the other checks use, the EPUBCheck version and "its messages are in English", and a switch "Check the EPUB after every export" (`validateEpub` in `state/view/editorPrefs.ts`, an app preference, off by default). With the switch on, an EPUB export keeps the dialog open and raises `useEpubCheckSignal` (`app/export/epubAutoCheck.ts`) so the result is seen. The button checks the last exported file of the shown edition; with no exported file it says to export first (it does not export for you). EPUBCheck's own message text is not translated.

**Rejected:** relying on the author's Java (most authors have none, and the point is no install step); a JavaScript or WebAssembly port (none is complete or maintained to EPUBCheck's level); a network validator service (the no-network rule); downloading the runtime on first use (also a network call); shipping the full JDK (about 300 MB; `jlink` gives 57 MB); a Settings > Export section for the switch (the app has no such section, and the switch is only useful next to the check).

**Trade-offs and caveats:** the installer grows by about 90 MB on Linux and about 180 MB on macOS universal (two runtimes; estimates from the staged sizes, not yet measured on a built installer). The JVM takes 3 to 6 seconds to start, so the check shows progress and never blocks a normal export. The runtime is per CPU and must be code-signed with the hardened runtime and notarized on macOS; a JVM needs the JIT entitlements (`com.apple.security.cs.allow-jit`, `allow-unsigned-executable-memory`), and that signing is not set up or tested yet (see `docs/release-checklist.md`). `scripts/build-jre.sh --all` downloads Temurin 17 archives and needs their pinned sha256 in `pin_for`, which are still to be filled in. The default run uses the JDK that `java_home` or `JAVA_HOME` points at.

## Back-of-book index and glossary: marks in the text, settings in the manifest, pages generated at export (2026-11-10, format 14)

**Decision:** an index entry is an inline `indexEntry` mark (`id`, `term`, optional `sub`, `see` or `seeAlso`) and a use of a glossary term is a `glossaryTerm` mark (`id` of an entry). The index is never stored: `collectIndexMarks` and `buildIndex` in `packages/shared/src/indexing/` read the marks and build it (sorted for the book's language, nested, grouped by letter) whenever it is needed, so editing the text can never leave a stale index. The manifest holds only what is not derivable: `index` (heading, columns, page ranges, letter headings, hidden terms) and `glossary` (the entries with their definitions and aliases, and its settings), formatVersion 14. The exporters write the two pages themselves, at the end of the book (index, then glossary), from a built `IndexPageSpec` and `GlossaryPageSpec`. The EPUB (`packages/export-epub/src/generated.ts`) and the PDF (`packages/export-pdf/src/typst/generated.ts`, page numbers from Typst `query` like the contents page) do this today; Markdown, Word and RTF write the same two pages as plain lists of page titles (`generatedDocPages` in `packages/shared/src/indexing/docs.ts`), since they have no page numbers to print. The EPUB: numbered links back to an anchor the chapter writes at the marked words, `epub:type="index"` and `"glossary"`, nav and landmark entries, and the first use of each glossary term in a chapter linked to its entry (only when that entry has a definition), with the entry linking back only to a link that was actually written.

**Why:** the marks keep each place attached to its words through edits, copy and paste, and splitting or merging chapters, which a list of positions cannot. Deriving the index at export time is how the contents page already works, and means the same data feeds the app's panel and every format. A glossary definition is the author's writing, so unlike the index it is stored, once, in the manifest and shared by every use.

| Rejected                                                                      | Why not                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| A zero-width inline node for each entry                                       | Every exporter's inline loop, search, word count and narration would need to special-case it, and it can be deleted by accident with a keystroke.                                                                                               |
| A sidecar list of entries with text offsets (like `comments/`)                | Offsets drift on every edit; the sidecar and the text would disagree after the first merge or paste.                                                                                                                                            |
| New `index` and `glossary` back-matter kinds (reversed 2026-11-11, see below) | About 30 files handle the generated `contents` kind (editor, sidebar, menus, templates, every exporter) for little gain; settings in the manifest need none of that. A chapter-list entry can be added later if authors want to place the page. |
| Store the built index in the manifest                                         | It would change on every edit and could go stale; it is cheap to rebuild.                                                                                                                                                                       |

**Trade-offs and caveats:** the generated pages always sit at the end of the book (after the back matter), not at a place the author chooses. The new words the EPUB writes ("See", "See also") have machine-drafted translations that need native review (see the roadmap). A mark inside a footnote is anchored in the endnotes section.

## Index and glossary as back-matter pages the author places (2026-11-11, format 15)

**Decision:** `index` and `glossary` are back-matter page kinds (`BACK_MATTER_KINDS`, single use), added from the Add page menu and Insert > Back Matter like any back-matter page, and moved with the rest. The page holds no text of its own: every exporter writes the generated list in that page's place (EPUB `writeGenerated`, PDF `generatedPages`, Word and Rich Text through `placeGeneratedDocPages` in `packages/shared/src/indexing/docs.ts`, Markdown `generatedText`), and the app fills the page for the reader, Page view and the page measure in `readPages` (`withGeneratedPages`, `apps/desktop/src/lib/indexing/pageSpecs.ts`) and shows it read-only in the editor (`GeneratedPage`). Adding the page switches `index.enabled` or `glossary.enabled` on (`addMatter`), so the manifest settings stay the one place for heading, columns and the rest. A list with nothing to show leaves its page out of exports; a book with the list on and no page still gets it at the end, so version 14 books export as before. The format is 15 because an older app would refuse the new kinds.

**Why:** this was the follow-up the first decision named ("a chapter-list entry can be added later if authors want to place the page"): authors want the index before an "About the author" page, or the glossary ahead of the index, and want to see the pages in the chapter list. The cost estimate (about 30 files) was high because the contents kind is handled by many places; these pages reuse the shared placement helpers and the contents kind's menus, so the change stayed small.

| Rejected                                                   | Why not                                                                                              |
| ---------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| A separate list of generated pages next to the back matter | A second ordering to move, rename and undo; the back-matter list already does all of it.             |
| Storing the list in the page file                          | It would go stale on every edit; the page file stays an ordinary, empty chapter file.                |
| Editing the generated text in the page                     | Typing there would be lost on the next export; the list is changed in the Index and Glossary panels. |

## Moving the designs to OpenPencil (2026-11-13)

**Decision:** the UI designs are `design/cadabracraft.fig`, edited in [OpenPencil](https://openpencil.dev) (MIT: desktop app, web app, `openpencil` CLI and an MCP server), not in pen.dev's Pencil. `docs/design.md` mirrors that file. The old `cadabracraft.pen` is kept unchanged in `design/archive/` and is not edited any more. `@open-pencil/cli` and `@open-pencil/core` are root dev dependencies (MIT, never shipped in an app); `pnpm design:from-pen` reruns the one-time conversion into `out/design/` (it never touches `design/`).

**Why:** Pencil and the `pen` CLI need an account and have a plan usage limit that blocked design work, and the file could only be read through their tools. OpenPencil needs neither, and its `.fig` can be read, rendered, linted and edited from a terminal or an agent.

**How the conversion works** (`scripts/design/`, unit tested): OpenPencil opens a `.pen` read-only and its `.fig` writer assumes a Figma-made file, so a direct convert came out scrambled (no layout, "Button" labels, dark frames light, empty squares for icons). `prepare.mjs` fixes this in order: (1) the pen's `icon` nodes, which OpenPencil does not know, become one component per lucide icon (`Icon / <name>`, shapes from `lucide-react`) with instance swaps for instances that show another icon; (2) text sizes bound to variables (`"$type-body"`, 16,513 of 17,557 texts) are resolved to numbers; (3) each frame's `theme` is applied as a variable mode and bound colours are resolved again; (4) `layoutAuthoredNodes` computes the sizes and positions the layout implies, which a `.pen` does not store; (5) the fields in which an instance differs from its component are recorded as overrides, because the writer only writes recorded ones. An instance the writer cannot address is detached to a frame and the export retried.

**Checked:** reloaded in the OpenPencil CLI, the Editor (light and dark), Sponsor dialog (light and dark) and website Home (dark) frames match the old design; the file has 58,275 nodes, 184 components and 38 variables in light and dark; editing a text with `openpencil eval -w`, saving and rendering again keeps layout, icons and dark mode; the Sponsor button label, icons and per-instance icon choices (about 3,000 swaps) survive.

| Rejected                                              | Why not                                                                                                                                                    |
| ----------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `openpencil convert` on the pen as is                 | Fails on one instance and, even with that fixed, loses layout, text sizes, overrides, themes and icons (see above).                                        |
| Keeping the pen as source and exporting to OpenPencil | Every edit would need Pencil and its plan again; the export is lossy, so the OpenPencil copy could never be edited. This was the first plan and was tried. |
| Detaching every instance to a frame                   | Looks right but throws away the components (5,014 instances), so a change to a Button would no longer reach its uses.                                      |
| Writing the `.fig` bytes or a Figma plugin ourselves  | OpenPencil's own writer, plus the five fixes above, is far less code to own.                                                                               |

**Trade-offs and caveats:** the file is a binary (about 1 MB), so a design change is a binary diff; review it by exporting frames to PNG. Known gaps: the `github` icon has no lucide shape and is an empty frame; one instance (`The Harbour`, `Section / Contents`) is a plain frame; some spacing is a little looser than the old file because layout used estimated text sizes (the app re-measures on edit); the website logo shape renders glitchy. OpenPencil is at 0.16 and says it has rough edges: if a change to its file format breaks the file, the pen in `design/archive/` and `pnpm design:from-pen` rebuild it. OpenPencil substitutes Inter for EB Garamond, JetBrains Mono, Libre Baskerville and Lora unless installed.

## Contact and feedback forms (2026-11-14)

**Decision:** Settings has a Help group with **Contact** and **Give feedback** buttons. Each opens a dialog with the matching form from the support desk (`https://support.cadabracraft.com/embed/contact` or `/embed/feedback`) in an `<iframe>`. The website's Support page has the contact form inline.

**Network rule:** the app asks the support desk for nothing until the author opens one of the two dialogs, so this is a third author-triggered exception next to the update check and the voice and font downloads (CLAUDE.md lists it). No cookies, no analytics, no referrer (`referrerPolicy="no-referrer"`); the frame is sandboxed. The frame's `load` event is not trusted (a page the desk refuses to frame still fires it, empty): the form counts as shown when it sends its `supdesk:height` message. If none arrives within 12 seconds, or the computer is offline, the dialog says so and offers "Open in browser" (`bridge.openUrl`). While it waits, the frame stays in place but invisible, with a "Loading the form…" status (spinner, `role="status"`, no animation under reduced motion) over it, so the dialog does not jump when the form appears. The dialog has no privacy footnote; the network rule above is documented here and in CLAUDE.md. Messages from the frame are used only for its height, and only when they come from that frame and from the support origin (`formHeightFrom`, unit tested).

**What changed in the app's security setup:** `frame-src` in `tauri.conf.json` (production and dev CSP) is `https://support.cadabracraft.com` instead of `'none'`; every other CSP directive is unchanged (so `connect-src` still lets the app itself call nothing). The navigation guard in `lib.rs` also allows `https://support.cadabracraft.com/embed/...`, because WKWebView reports a frame's navigation to the same handler as the window's. It is the exact host over https with no port and only the `/embed/` path; any other address still counts as refused (Rust tests).

**Website:** `ContactForm` adds the desk's own `embed.js` (`data-supdesk="contact"`) on the Support page only, with a plain `<iframe>` inside `<noscript>`. The support subdomain is the project's own service, but it is a separate origin, so "the site makes no third-party requests" is true of every page except Support. No CSP on GitHub Pages, so nothing else changes.

**Rejected:**

| Alternative                                        | Why not                                                                                                     |
| -------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `embed.js` inside the app                          | Needs `script-src` for a remote host, which would let remote code run with the app's IPC rights.            |
| Loading the form frame when Settings opens         | A network request the author did not ask for.                                                               |
| A `mailto:` link                                   | No mail client on many computers, and no way to attach the app version or reply address without extra work. |
| Building the form in the app and posting to an API | A network call from the app's own code (`connect-src`), and a second form to keep in step with the desk.    |
| Widening the navigation guard to the whole host    | Only `/embed/` pages are needed.                                                                            |

**Known limit:** keys pressed while the form has focus go to the form, so Escape does not close the dialog from inside it; the Close button does, and Escape works from anywhere else in the dialog.

## Selecting tests from the change: a deterministic map in shadow mode first (2026-11-15)

**Decision:** pull requests will run only the parts of CI a change can affect, chosen by `scripts/affected/affected.mjs` from `git diff` and the workspace's `package.json` files (no model). Today it runs in **shadow mode**: the `changes` job prints what it would select in the job summary and every job still runs in full. It is enforced only after about two weeks (or 20 pull requests) in which it missed no test that failed in the full run. Enforcement also needs a nightly full run on `main`, the `full-ci` label (already read by the script) and the full coverage gate on `main`, because the coverage floors can only be measured on a full run.

**Rules:** a docs, design or agent-settings change needs no test; a change in `src-tauri` selects the Rust job and the WebDriver build; a change in a package selects that package, everything that depends on it and, when the desktop app is among them, the Playwright and accessibility runs; `apps/website` stays in its own package. Lockfiles, workflows, `scripts/`, the Playwright and Vite configs, the e2e support code and root configs, any file no rule knows, a diff that cannot be read and the `full-ci` label select everything.

**Not built yet:** a per-test file map for Playwright (the part that would cut the hour-long run). The app's Vite dev server loads the core of the app in every test, so a change to a core file would still select almost every test; only changes to a leaf component would shrink the run. Build it from the coverage data we already collect only if an estimate over the last commits shows a median selection well under half of the suite.

**Rejected:**

| Alternative                                   | Why not                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| --------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Meta-style predictive selection or Launchable | Needs weeks of test history and a model to retrain, or an external service that gets the repository's metadata.                                                                                                                              |
| An LLM picking tests from the diff            | The safe designs (PyTorch, Juju) wrap it in a deterministic ranking, shadow mode and a run-everything fallback; the model would add a secret and send the diff out for little gain over the map. It can be added later as an add-only layer. |
| Playwright `--only-changed`                   | Selects changed spec files only: a source change selects nothing, a config change selects hundreds.                                                                                                                                          |
| Vitest `--changed` alone                      | Follows static imports only and reruns everything on a config change; the package graph is exact for this monorepo and also covers the other jobs.                                                                                           |

**Known limit:** an unmapped or global change always runs everything, so the saving is for the common small changes (docs, one package, one Rust module, one component), not for a change to shared setup.

## One port per CI runner for the browser and WebDriver suites (2026-11-16)

**Decision:** the CI jobs that start a server (`js`, `a11y`, `webdriver`) set `E2E_PORT` and `TAURI_DRIVER_PORT` from the runner's number (the `N` of the runner name `...-rN`): `1420+N` and `4444+2N`. The four runners of one machine share its network and run one job each, so each job gets ports no other job on the machine can use; a runner name without a number keeps the defaults. In CI the Playwright and coverage output folders keep their plain names (a runner has its own checkout, and `scripts/coverage.mjs` reads `coverage/e2e`); locally a run with its own `E2E_PORT` still gets `-<port>` folders.

**Why:** the Coverage step of `js` (about an hour) and the `a11y` job (about 45 minutes) overlap on one machine, and both started the dev server on 1420; the second failed with "http://localhost:1420 is already used" (run #172). The WebDriver suite had the same fixed-port problem on 4444.

**Rejected:**

| Alternative                                           | Why not                                                                                                                                                                            |
| ----------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Probe for a free port at start                        | Playwright loads its config in the main process and in every worker, and they must all compute the same port; a probe gives each a different answer, and it races with other jobs. |
| `reuseExistingServer` in CI                           | Would silently test the other job's dev server, that is, another checkout's code.                                                                                                  |
| A lock so Playwright jobs on a machine run one by one | Works, but adds the length of the shorter job (up to an hour) to the wall time of every run.                                                                                       |

**Known limit:** the rule rests on the runner names. A runner pool that names runners differently falls back to the default ports, and two jobs there could collide again; the failure is the same clear "port already used" message.

## Enforcing test selection and splitting the JavaScript job (2026-11-16)

**Decision:** the shadow selection of "Selecting tests from the change" now decides which CI jobs run on a pull request. The serial `js` job is split into `static`, `unit`, a 4-shard `e2e` and a `coverage` job; a `ci-ok` job is the single required check; repeated setup moved into composite actions (`.github/actions/ports`, `apt`, `setup-js`, `js-tools`). Pull requests run Playwright without V8 coverage. The coverage floors are checked on `main`, release runs and pull requests with `full-ci`. The macOS unit leg runs on full runs only.

**Why:** a pull request waited for one job that ran every check in series, then about 3000 Playwright tests with coverage in one process (about an hour), whatever it changed. The floors themselves are unchanged; only where they are checked moved.

**Rejected:**

| Alternative                                       | Why not                                                                                                                                                           |
| ------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Sharding the coverage run and merging the V8 data | Not done yet: the merge cannot be tried without a CI run. The `coverage` job stays one job on full runs; do it when the shard timings show the hour is the limit. |
| `paths-ignore` for documentation changes          | A skipped workflow leaves a required check pending; the selector plus `ci-ok` stays green when jobs are skipped.                                                  |
| Time-balanced shards from recorded durations      | Needs recorded timings first; Playwright's own `--shard` (with `fullyParallel`) splits by test count until then.                                                  |
| Keeping the floors as a pull-request gate         | It forces the hour-long instrumented run onto every change; the `full-ci` label runs it when a change adds or moves code.                                         |

**Known limit:** a code change merged without `full-ci` can lower coverage until the next push to `main` fails the floor; the fix is then tests, as always. Typecheck and lint run on Linux only (the macOS `js` leg ran them too).

## Narrower pull-request runs: scripts, validators, tools and static checks (2026-11-19)

**Decision:** the selection of "Enforcing test selection" got finer. Only the `ci` and `release` workflows, the shared actions and most of `scripts/` are global triggers; the perf, fuzz, design and release-only scripts, the `pages` and `perf-fuzz` workflows, the perf and fuzz crates under `src-tauri`, `brand/`, dependabot and git-hook files select no test job. A new `website` job runs `next build` when the website changes. The `unit` job installs the external tools (EPUBCheck, Ace, veraPDF, LibreOffice, enwik8) only when a change reaches a package that drives one, and the `a11y` job splits into exporter validators (when an exporter is reached) and page sweeps (WebKit, when the app's pages are reached). On a pull request that is not a full run, `static` typechecks the changed packages and their dependents (`pnpm --filter "...[base]"`) and runs eslint and prettier on the changed files (`scripts/affected/static-checks.sh`); size and the scripts' tests still see the whole tree.

**Why:** a change to the app's pages paid for LibreOffice, veraPDF, Ace and Java it never uses, a perf-script edit ran the whole suite, and a docs or one-package change still linted and formatted the whole repository.

**Rejected:**

| Alternative                                         | Why not                                                                                                                        |
| --------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Mapping app sources to the specs that cover them    | Nothing declares it, and a missed spec is a missed regression; the e2e job still narrows only for spec-only changes (roadmap). |
| Skipping `static` altogether for non-code changes   | It is the cheap check that catches formatting and size; it now scales with the change instead.                                 |
| Making the desktop package one of the tool packages | Its tests use fakes; counting it would turn the tools on for almost every change and save nothing.                             |

**Known limit:** a lint rule that looks at other files than the one linted (none today) would be missed by changed-file linting until the next full run on `main`.

**Follow-up (2026-11-19): e2e narrowing.** The `e2e` job now narrows for app sources and packages too: the specs that import a changed e2e helper (import graph) plus the specs that called a function in a changed source file (`apps/desktop/e2e/spec-map.json`, written by the instrumented run with `SPEC_MAP=1`). Unit tests and Markdown select no spec. A file the map and graph do not know runs all specs; full runs and pushes to `main` run everything and the `coverage` job flags a stale map. Rejected: running only the specs whose names match a feature folder (silent misses), and `playwright --only-changed` (follows spec imports only, never app sources).

## Heavy CI jobs back on the self-hosted runners (2026-11-19)

**Decision:** in `ci.yml`, the four `e2e` shards, `coverage`, `a11y`, `webdriver` and the Linux leg of `rust` run on `kinhin-container`, and the macOS legs of `unit` and `rust` on `kinhin-xcode`, except that a pull request's macOS `rust` leg stays on `blacksmith-6vcpu-macos-latest`: `kinhin-xcode` holds the Developer ID certificate and the notary key, so a pull request's code (a fork's, a dependency bump's build script) must not run on it. The macOS `unit` leg never runs on pull requests, so it only ever sees `main` and release code. The quick jobs stay on Blacksmith: `changes`, `static`, `website`, the Linux `unit` leg, `licenses` and `ci-ok`.

**Why:** Blacksmith's usage page showed the long jobs as the bill: `e2e` $9.76 (93 runs), `rust` $7.87 (52), `a11y` $6.38 (22), `coverage` $6.24 (8 runs, about $0.78 each), `webdriver` $3.42 (32) and `unit` $2.67 (29). The moved jobs are long, run one at a time per workflow run and nobody waits on a few extra minutes for most of them (`coverage` and the macOS legs run on full runs only; `webdriver` is `continue-on-error`); the `e2e` shards followed because they were the largest cost. The `-rN` runner names already give each runner its own ports, cargo and rustup folders, so the shared-machine setup from before still works.

**Rejected:**

| Alternative                            | Why not                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| -------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Keeping the `e2e` shards on Blacksmith | They were the biggest line ($9.76), so they moved too (2026-11-19, a second step). Four shards plus the other moved jobs now share the machine's runner slots, so a busy moment queues them; the per-runner ports keep them apart. |
| Moving every job back                  | The self-hosted container was the slowest and least predictable part of CI (a runner was lost mid-`rust` job on the v0.1.0 run), so the quick jobs keep Blacksmith's speed.                                                        |

**Known limit:** `kinhin-container` runs pull-request code too; it holds no signing material and builds no release artifact (`release.yml` builds on `blacksmith-8vcpu-ubuntu-2404` and `kinhin-xcode`, whose Cargo and Rustup folders pull requests never touch), so what a pull request leaves in the container's per-runner Cargo and Rustup folders can at most skew a later CI verdict, including the `ci` gate a release waits on, never the shipped binaries, but keep "Require approval for outside collaborators' workflow runs" on in the repository settings so a fork's code does not run unreviewed on either self-hosted machine. `kinhin-xcode` stopped picking up jobs on 2026-11-19 and left runs waiting, which blocked `ci-ok`. If a moved job queues for long, change its label back to the Blacksmith one (one line per job; the previous labels are `blacksmith-8vcpu-ubuntu-2404` and `blacksmith-6vcpu-macos-latest`). Several moved jobs at once queue behind the machine's runner slots, and a long queue shows up as a slow pull request, not a failure.
